# PROJECT STATE — the single source of truth

**This document is the ONE place that states where the project actually is.**
If another document disagrees with this one, this one wins and the other is a bug.

| document | role |
|---|---|
| **`PROJECT_STATE.md`** (this file) | **where we ARE** — measured position, open ledger, corrections |
| `ROADMAP.md` | where we INTEND to go — strategy, tracks, principles |
| `AUDIT_R7_FIX_PLAN.md` | one programme's execution record (round-7). Historical |
| `corpora/*/manifest.json`, `corpora/real_roots/results.json` | the artefacts that OWN the numbers |

**Why this file exists.** Before it, the most accurate picture of this project
lived in three places a future reader could not reach: an agent's private memory,
ephemeral `/tmp` workflow outputs, and PR descriptions. The repo itself carried a
ROADMAP whose banner, false-READY count, over-rejection claim and
version-of-record were each factually false while `check_roadmap_facts.py`
printed `Roadmap facts check passed`. Measured on 2026-08-22, **no in-repo prose
carried the real-paper measurement at all**, and the strings `declared_texlive`,
`rescue-alone` and the `corpora/unicode` finding appeared in **zero** files.

**The rules that keep it true.**

1. Numbers in the generated block below come from the artefact that owns them.
   `check_project_state.py` regenerates and diffs. **Never hand-edit that block.**
2. Every open item has a stable `OPEN-nnn` id. Cite it in the PR that closes it.
   Ids are never reused.
3. Every claim carries provenance: a file:line, a command, or `UNVERIFIED`.
4. When a belief turns out to be wrong, it goes in §4 — the corrections log.
   That section is the point of this document, not an appendix to it.

---

### Strict-tier coverage — THE North-Star metric (ADR-012)

A document counts only when the CLI prints a **PROVEN** verdict (READY or NOT-READY, decided inside the contract-bounded strict tier by the Coq-extracted decider) **and** that verdict matches the pinned pdflatex. `strict_wrong` counts every PROVEN verdict that disagrees with pdflatex; ADR-012 also counts a wrong reason or location, which the strict battery and the generated differential grade. It must be zero, and it is published with its exact one-sided 95% upper bound, because zero out of a small number is weak evidence. **In milestone M0 the strict-tier membership predicate is a stub that returns false, so no verdict is proven and this number is zero by measurement.** The North Star is defined on a VIRGIN sample, and that is sample 3 (frame offset 720, ranks 721-920), drawn and graded once, under the frozen oracle (CI's digest-pinned TeX Live image, ADR-012 decision 7), after every earlier graded artefact had been re-graded under it (OPEN-118). It is SEALED for measurement only (OPEN-119): nothing is fixed, tuned or triaged on it, and a future fix is validated elsewhere before sample 3 is re-measured. Samples 1 and 2 are shown for comparison and are not virgin: sample 1 is tuned and sample 2 has been design-seen since ADR-012. Definitions: `docs/v27/STRICT_TIER_DESIGN.md` §E and `docs/v27/adr/ADR-012-contract-bounded-proven-tier.md`.

| corpus | strict-tier coverage (PROVEN = pdflatex) | strict_wrong | 95% upper bound on the strict_wrong rate | heuristic verdicts | foreign verdicts |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| sample 1 (tuned) | **0/200 = 0.0%** | 0 | n/a — no proven verdicts, so no evidence either way | 199 | 1 |
| sample 2 (design-seen since ADR-012) | **0/200 = 0.0%** | 0 | n/a — no proven verdicts, so no evidence either way | 197 | 3 |
| **sample 3 (VIRGIN; sealed, OPEN-119)** | **0/200 = 0.0%** | 0 | n/a — no proven verdicts, so no evidence either way | 198 | 2 |

### Heuristic-tier statistic: premise-certified coverage (NOT a proof)

**This is a heuristic-tier statistic, not the North Star and not a proof (ADR-012, decision 2).** Before ADR-012 it was published as the North-Star metric under the name *proven-verdict coverage*. It counts documents where the Coq-extracted checker certified its PREMISES over the abstract model (`PREMISE-CERTIFIED`) **and** pdflatex compiled the document; the CLI renders every such verdict as `LIKELY OK (heuristic; premise-certified)`. It is NOT a proof that the document compiles: the second table below gives how often that reading is wrong, computed from the same artefacts. Restricting to LP-Core does not reliably reduce it — the direction differs between the two samples, so no general claim is made either way (C-43 withdrew the earlier one). The LP-Core column is the heuristic figure this project publishes, and only under this heading.

| corpus | premise-certified (LP-Core) | certified (any tier) | uncertified READYs | certified FALSE-READY |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| sample 1 (tuned) | 100/200 = 50.0% | 185/200 = 92.5% | 0 | 0 |
| **sample 2 (untuned; design-seen since ADR-012)** | 88/200 = 44.0% | 173/200 = 86.5% | 0 | 7 |
| **sample 3 (VIRGIN; sealed, OPEN-119)** | 88/200 = 44.0% | 177/200 = 88.5% | 1 | 4 |

## 2. Where we are, in one paragraph

The verification substrate is now trustworthy and the fixer is safe. The gates
bind (required contexts include `build` and `tex-oracle`, and real pdflatex runs
in CI against a digest-pinned image). The fixer's measured damage is zero on the
round-trip corpus, and both of its byte channels are screened. What changed the
picture is that the tool was graded against real documents for the first time:
the accuracy is much worse than the fixture corpora suggested, and **the dominant
failure mode is over-rejection, not the cardinal bug**. The R7 programme is
complete as designed and superseded as strategy — it moved fixture baseline (b)
from 21 to 7 while the published banner moved 35 → 34, because (a), (b) and (c)
are disjoint corpora. The next phase is accuracy work, and most of it is not in
the ROADMAP.

**2026-09-26 — ADR-012: nothing this project prints is a proof yet, and the
headline now says so.** The North Star is redefined as **strict-tier coverage**:
the share of papers in a virgin sample that get a PROVEN verdict from the
contract-bounded strict tier and on which that verdict matches pdflatex, at zero
`strict_wrong`. The strict tier does not exist yet — milestone M0 ships its
verdict type with the membership predicate stubbed to false — so the generated
North-Star row above reads zero on both samples, **by measurement**: the CLI
records its tier token per paper and no paper carries the proven one. The figure
that used to be published as the North Star (premise-certified coverage) is
still published, under a heading that names it a **heuristic-tier statistic**,
together with how often its certificate is wrong. Every READY the CLI prints now
reads `LIKELY OK (heuristic; premise-certified) — not a proof`; no exit code
moved. The programme, its milestones and the measured boundary of the strict
grammar on sample 2 are ledger row **OPEN-116**; the decision record is
[ADR-012](adr/ADR-012-contract-bounded-proven-tier.md) and the design is
[STRICT_TIER_DESIGN.md](STRICT_TIER_DESIGN.md).

**2026-09-27 — the oracle is frozen, and freezing it moved nothing.** Every
pdflatex grade now goes through `scripts/tools/_oracle.py`, which runs CI's
digest-pinned TeX Live image (a container on a workstation, the image itself in
CI) and never a host `pdflatex`. The maintainer's laptop had printed the pinned
banner while its package set differed from the image's in 190 packages, so the
old banner check pinned the engine binary and nothing else. Every graded
artefact was re-graded once under the image; the re-grade moved no cell on
either real-paper sample, the three fixer windows, the strict battery, the
false-READY fixtures or the round-trip corpus. One recorded failure reason was
the laptop's own damage (C-74). Ledger row **OPEN-118** carries the numbers, the
per-artefact diffs, the four research artefacts not re-graded, and the one
command that draws sample 3 at the window the owner's delegate chose, with
what the check of that window found.

**2026-09-27 — sample 3, the virgin sample, is drawn and sealed.** It was
graded once, at a `main` commit, under the pinned image, and the generated
block now publishes it as the North Star's virgin row beside the two samples
that are not virgin. It is sealed for measurement only: nothing is fixed,
tuned or triaged on it, and a fix is validated on other documents before it is
re-measured here. Ledger row **OPEN-119** carries the seal, the harness check
and what the recording run itself printed.

**2026-09-04, restated 2026-09-12: this slot publishes NO numbers. Read the generated block above.** It used to carry a hand-written matrix that contradicted the generated block fifty lines above it — the single-source-of-truth document disagreeing with itself (C-40). Hand-written positional numbers are BANNED here, and as of 2026-09-12 that ban is MACHINERY rather than a promise: `check_doc_consistency.py`'s `inv_no_handwritten_position` now fails on any fraction or percentage in this file's running prose, anchored on the quantity shape rather than on a phrasing, with no keyword exemption. ⚠ **The previous version of this paragraph declared the ban and then broke it in the same sentence, and the gate could not see it** — the exemption was keyed on the word `superseded`, which this paragraph opened with, so four stale figures sat here for eight days while every required context stayed green (OPEN-078, C-47). Markdown table rows remain exempt, because the ledger and the corrections log must be able to quote what a number used to be, and each such row is dated and carries its own evidence cell. **The two readings that must always travel together — the tuned sample and the VIRGIN sample — are the first two rows of the generated block. Quoting the tuned one alone is the error C-39 exists to stop.**

⚠ **Baseline (b) reads 14, not 7, and that is not a regression.** R7 drove the
fixtures it owned from 21 down to 7. The other 7 are the `corpora/unicode`
false-READYs (OPEN-001): they were live the whole time, in a corpus **no gate
covered**, so the published 7 was an artefact of what was being counted rather
than of what was broken. Gating them raised the *number* and lowered the
*unknown*. Expect this again — a baseline that only ever falls is measuring its
own scope.

**2026-09-29 — ADR-015: the proven tier is static, real-time and engine-free, and it will stand on
a Coq translation of the pinned pdfTeX.** After four adversarial review rounds of the per-name
command-signature track (OPEN-120) each found a new composition hole (next-token swallowing; the
mode a use leaves behind; `x \section{a}\par\unskip x`; `\label` storing a counter's printed form:
C-82, C-90, recorded on the unmerged branch `feat/v27165-contract-signatures`), the owner stopped the
signature track and chose the synthesis: pdfTeX's own program, translated into Coq, is the fixed foundation
(one premise, `FaithfulEngine`, per engine revision, image and architecture); commands are admitted
offline by a Coq-proven sound abstract interpreter over their real definitions; the fast static kernel
decides in real time. A two-week foundation spike with kill criteria is funded (OPEN-123). Its first
step, H.1, identified the pinned binary's exact source revision and rebuilt both architectures' binaries
byte for byte ([report](spike/H1-report.md), evidence in [`spike/h1/`](https://github.com/ClanClanClanClan/latex_perf/tree/6988d649a02be825729c2eefcbf3c89f9de7b0bb/docs/v27/spike/h1) on the spike branch at `6988d649`). The decision record is
[ADR-015](adr/ADR-015-static-proven-tier-on-translated-engine.md).

LaTeX Error: Unicode character … not set up for use with LaTeX`), and they are now in `corpora/false_ready/manifest.json` via `../unicode/` paths — baseline **8 → 14**, oracle **29 fixtures, hard=0 soft=0**. `CHAR-021` confirmed vacuous (CLI NOT-READY, pdflatex COMPILES). ⚠ **It is NOT a detection gap.** All six rules FIRE — `CHAR-012`/`CHAR-019` at `info`, `CHAR-014`/`ENC-007`/`ENC-019`/`TYPO-053` at `warning` — and **`ENC-007`/`ENC-019` are ALREADY in `compile_blocking_ids`**. T5 is `r.severity = Error && is_compile_blocking r.id` (`compile_contract.ml:285,308`), so **severity**, not detection, is what lets them through. ⚠ **Naive promotion is measurably unsafe**: 29 in-frame real papers carry these codepoints and **24 COMPILE, with ZERO failing on the Unicode error** — promotion manufactures 24 over-rejections and rescues nothing (re-confirms the compile-blocking-promotion finding). ✅ **A CONTEXT-AWARE promotion is safe, and this was measured**: of **125 occurrences across those 29 toplevels — 112 sit in a `%` comment or a `comment` env, 6 inside `\begin{CJK}…\end{CJK}`, 5 in documents that `\newunicodechar`/`\DeclareUnicodeCharacter` the codepoint, 2 after `\end{document}`, and ZERO are live.** Established by controlled injection rather than inference: in each paper the codepoint is rc 0 where it sits and rc 1 when injected after `\begin{document}`. ⚠ Polarity is friendlier than OPEN-007 — an over-wide dead-region scan leaves today's false-READY unfixed, an under-wide one over-rejects, and **neither creates a NEW false-READY**. | **VERIFIED 2026-08-23** — 14 fixtures compiled, 29 papers compiled, 125 occurrences classified, injection controls | M |

It was a ONE-PASS-ORACLE ARTEFACT, and it is fixed.** ⚠ **Class corrected SOUND → INSTR.** All 3 papers (`2507.10419v1`, `2506.22536v1`, `2506.17405v1`) fail pass 1 with `! Package natbib Error: Bibliography not compatible with author-year citations.` and compile **rc 0 with a PDF on pass 2**, unedited, in the same directory — natbib cannot know the citation style until it has read the `.aux`/`.bbl` that pass 1 writes. Fixed, and `--refresh-metadata` backfills the manifest without re-running pdflatex (asserts every `sha256_tree` first) — **131/200 rows now carry a version**. ⚠ **The drift split the README mandated is impossible, not merely unfollowed**: `texlive_version` is present on 1,880 of 2,821 packages and **every declared value is 2023** — not one paper declares the oracle's TL2026, so "restrict to `declared_texlive == 2026`" selects the EMPTY SET. There is no same-engine stratum; the confound is universal. The only split the corpus supports shows **no signal**: false-READY is **7/130 (5.4%)** among TL2023-declaring papers vs **4/69 (5.8%)** among undeclared. So "a precision fix that keeps catching the 4" is **not achievable** — the 4 need real detectors, and a T2 precision fix should be judged purely on the 13. ⚠ Only **13 of the 17** are comment-driven; the other 4 are three separate previously-unrecorded mechanisms (directory shadowing, kpathsea resolution, duplicate include) that deserve their own rows. | **VERIFIED 2026-08-24** — 18/18 recompiled, 18/18 sha-matched, mechanism read per document | M |

| OPEN-018 | SOUND | **OR-1 / DWR-1**: `validators_common.ml:623-638` takes the byte after `\lstinline` as the delimiter, guarded only by "not a letter", so an optional argument makes `[` the delimiter. THREE distinct defects, and the trigger is narrower than recorded. **(a) CORRUPTION**, needs a `[` inside the option VALUE: `\lstinline[caption={[x]}]\|a--b\|` → the fixer rewrites the verbatim to `a–b`. **(b) SILENT OVER-REACH**, the common case: with no later `[` the range runs to EOF, so every fix in the REST of the document is withheld — verified, `prose -- here` after an `\lstinline[language=C]` is never fixed. **(c) BLANK-SKIP**: the delimiter is read at a fixed offset, but TeX absorbs a space after a control word — `X \verb |ab| Y` compiles (rc 0) with `|` as the delimiter, while the scanner takes the SPACE. Any fix must skip blanks in ONE shared helper used by both the scanner and `verb_broken_eol_fatal`, or the re-key re-implements the same wrong rule. Plain `\lstinline\|a--b\|` is correct. Named blocker on any fixer-soundness claim and prerequisite for OPEN-006/007. ✅ **ADDRESSED — but it took TWO passes, and the first one made things worse.** #545 fixed (a)(b)(c) and simultaneously **(d) INTRODUCED A REGRESSION**: the `[`-consumption was applied to `\verb` too, which has NO optional argument and for which `[` is a legal delimiter (`\verb[a--b[` compiles, rc 0). The scan walked past the real closing delimiter onto a letter and recorded **no range at all**, so the fixer rewrote the VERBATIM BODY `a -- b` → `a – b` — the same corruption as (a), in the sibling command. It also **(e) MISSED THE MOST COMMON FORM**: `\lstinline{code}` is a brace group closed by the matching `}`, not a character delimiter closed by a SECOND `{`, so the range over-reached to the next `{` and silently withheld every fix in between — **30 of 67 corpus occurrences, against 22 for the `[` form it did fix**. Both closed here, pinned by 5 new `test_exempt_ranges` cases; false-READY baseline and the 35/20/8 differential both unchanged. ⚠ Measured incidence of (c) and (d) is **ZERO in 8,602 corpus files**; (e) is the only member of this family with real incidence. See [OPEN-023] for the part still open. | **VERIFIED 2026-08-23** by reproduction, then by fix | M |

| OPEN-056 | GATE | ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-10-02 by OPEN-125** (mode (a) is a fork-inherited socket end hanging the retirement that a units bug forced on every request; mode (b) is the dead-worker/rescue path, rewritten there; this harness, 10 runs: 23 attempt-failures on `origin/main`, 0 fixed). The history below is kept. ⚠ **RE-DIAGNOSED 2026-09-12, AND THE PREVIOUS DIAGNOSIS IS REFUTED IN THREE PLACES. It is now REPRODUCIBLE ON DEMAND IN ~10 SECONDS: `bash scripts/repro_proxy_smoke_local.sh`.** Class corrected TRACK -> GATE. **(1) It is not load-dependent and not 1-in-40. It is DETERMINISTIC**: on `origin/main` the local harness produces EXACTLY 2 `attempt=1 status=1` failures in 10 runs out of 10, zero variance. The published "39 of 40 runs pass" measured the RETRY POLICY — `proxy_smoke.py` retries 5x — not the defect; the per-attempt failure rate is 2 of 3, every run. **(2) There are TWO failure modes, and the recorded one is the RARE one.** ⚠ **This clause corrects an over-correction of my own, caught by CI within the hour — the first version said the HUP path "was never the mechanism", which is too strong.** Counted: **(a) request RECEIVED and never answered** — `[svc] recv req_id=... len=9` with no reply, the proxy timing out at 5 s. This is **5 of 5** local failures and the majority in CI. **(b) `hedged_call` RAISES** — `[svc] hedged_call exn`, which `main_service.ml:175` catches as `Failure _`, and the broker's only `failwith`s are `rescue failed (HUP)` and `rescue unexpected`. So the recorded HUP path IS exercised — **exactly ONCE per failing CI run** (runs 34719484000 and 34721495219), and **ZERO times in any local run**. Mode (a) dominates; mode (b) is real and rare. The trailing `read_exact(hdr) exn: Failure("unexpected EOF")` is benign in both: the proxy hanging up after its own timeout. **(3) It is not a tight timeout.** The proxy allows **5 SECONDS** (`rust/l0_lexer_client/src/lib.rs:53`) and a healthy request takes **4.5-7.3 ms**, so each failure is a multi-second STALL. 🔴 **THE RECORDED FIX SHAPE IS ALSO REFUTED, BY MEASUREMENT.** The proposed worker-readiness handshake was built in full — a `Warming` state, a dedicated readiness pipe so the Ipc framing is untouched, `promote`/`await_ready`, and a bounded wait used only when no worker is hot — and measured over the same 10 runs: **26 attempt-failures against the baseline's 20, and it introduced variance where there was none** (a steady 2 became 2-3). It was REVERTED, not landed. Do not rebuild it. ⚠ **AND THE DATA NOW EXPLAINS WHY IT BACKFIRED**, which is the useful part: the handshake targets the ROTATION / RESCUE path, i.e. mode (b), the one that fires once per run — while adding a `select` per worker to `poll_all` on EVERY `pick_hot`, i.e. on the hot path that mode (a) runs through. It made the common mode worse to address the rare one. **What is now RULED OUT: worker readiness at fork as the cause of mode (a).** ⚠ **A further clue from CI run 34721495219, and it argues against transient load: request 2 failed on ALL FIVE attempts** while request 1 succeeded on its second. Once a request enters the bad state it STAYS bad across five attempts spanning ~30 s, so mode (a) is a STUCK STATE, not jitter. What is not yet probed: why a service that has received a request takes >5 s to answer while its neighbours take 5 ms — `drain_one_ready`, the hedge timer, and the per-connection thread are the unexamined candidates, and the reproduction script makes each of them cheap to instrument. Instrument mode (a) first: it is 5 of 5 locally and needs no CI runner. | **VERIFIED 2026-09-12** — 10 baseline runs and 10 with the candidate fix, same machine, same harness; artefact evidence from CI run 34719484000 | M | Measured incidence: **39 of the last 40 runs pass**; exactly one needed a rerun, so this is NOT a chronically broken gate — but the failure is load-dependent, not random. Diagnosed from run 33959408910 attempt 1: `proxy.stderr` shows `[proxy] uds error for 9B: timeout` x5 and `service.stderr` shows `[svc] hedged_call exn len=5`. Chain: primary worker HUPs → secondary HUPs → `Broker.rescue_once`, which opens with `Array.iter (maybe_rotate p) p.workers` (rotating **every** worker) and then `pick_hot`s one and writes **immediately**; the write lands on a worker still in `mlock → gc → arenas`, HUPs, and `rescue_once` ends in `failwith "broker: rescue failed (HUP)"`, which `main_service.ml` catches as `` `Err `` → **status=1**. ⚠ **Root cause: `broker.ml`'s `type worker` has NO readiness field, and `maybe_rotate` sets `w.state <- Hot` the instant it forks** — the worker knows when it is up (it prints `ready, entering loop`) but the broker has no channel to learn it, so `pick_hot` can return a starting worker. Fast machines win the race; a loaded CI runner does not. ⚠ **Two red herrings, recorded so they are not re-chased**: `[svc] read_exact(hdr) exn: Failure("unexpected EOF")` is BENIGN (the smoke client opens one connection per attempt and closes it), and that day's `Cache service responded with 400` warnings were a real GitHub outage but did not cause this — build, service start and both readiness waits all succeeded, only step 12 failed. ⚠ **The failing requests are NOT in the step log**: the step `cat`s `service.stderr` BEFORE running the smoke, so diagnosis requires the `proxy-service-logs` artifact. Fix shape: a readiness handshake — leave a rotated slot `Warming` until the worker signals ready, and have `pick_hot` skip non-ready workers. Genuine concurrency change; do not ship speculatively. | **VERIFIED 2026-09-05** — artifact downloaded and read; chain traced through broker.ml rescue_once/maybe_rotate and main_service.ml's Failure handler; incidence measured over 40 runs | M |

ENC-008 advanced on the LEAD BYTE alone; 4 of its 5 corpus fires were false positives.** Both branches (`b0 = 0xEE`, `b0 = 0xEF`) incremented without checking that the next two bytes are UTF-8 continuations, so latin-1 `î` (0xEE) and `ï` (0xEF) counted as private-use codepoints. `CHAR-004` always had the guard (`validators_l1_expl3.ml:22-33`) and abstained correctly — which is why the plan's suggestion to delete CHAR-004 as "the Reserved duplicate" would have kept the WRONG rule. **Measured over all 2,961 corpus papers: 5 papers fired before, 1 after — 4 false positives removed** (`2506.15641v1`, `2507.00531v1`, `2507.06962v2`, `2507.08692v1`). Fixed by porting the continuation guard; 3 regression tests added (latin-1 `î`, latin-1 `ï`, and a real U+E000 among latin-1 noise), 162 cases pass. ⚠ Verdict-neutral by construction: ENC-008 emits `Warning`, and T5 requires `Error`, so it could never reach a readiness verdict (OPEN-058). ⚠ Same defect class as the language detector's byte scans, fixed the same day — **lead-byte-without-continuation is now a known recurring shape in this codebase; grep for it before trusting any byte scan.** | **VERIFIED 2026-09-06** — full-corpus byte sweep before/after; reproducers both directions | S |

| OPEN-061 | OVERREJ | **Language gating is wired but never invoked: 66.6% of real papers get findings from rules written for other languages.** Exactly **46** rules carry a non-empty `languages` list. `Validators.filter_by_language` (`validators.ml:710`) has exactly ONE caller, `run_all_for_language`, and that function's only four callers in the entire repo are **tests** — every production path iterates `get_rules ()` unfiltered. ⚠ **Measured on 939 real papers: 625 (66.6%) receive at least one cross-language finding**; 908 findings total, 35.0% of everything the 46 tagged rules emit. Worst offender `LANG-003` ("Mixed French/English punctuation spacing") fires on **552 of 939 = 58.8%**. ⚠ **The obvious fix is measurably unsafe**: routing `run_all` through `filter_by_language ... (detect_language src)` flips **25 golden `expect` entries across 3 suites**, puts 129 `run_all`-routed assertions at risk, and costs the required `smoke-cli` producer-coverage gate 21 rules' triggers — and the detector cannot carry the load anyway (the locale suite's own JA-001/KO-001 fixtures detect as `en`, JA-002's as `zh`). ✅ **DETECTOR FIXED 2026-09-05 — the prerequisite, landed alone.** Measurement INVERTED the order: over 409 real roots only **9.3%** carry a live declaration and **ZERO** declare French, so the gate correctly kills `LANG-003` (248 findings → 1) — but `detect_language` is wrong on **8.6%** of papers and **every one of the 68 true-positive losses traces to a detector defect, not the gate**. Both scans were COMMENT-BLIND (all 32 CJK-heuristic detections were driven by CJK inside `%` comments; 3 papers were read as declaring a language whose babel line existed only in a comment), and the byte helpers tested the LEAD byte only, so latin-1 `é` (0xE9) read as Chinese — **the same lead-byte-without-continuation defect as ENC-008/OPEN-059**. ⚠ The old behaviour was PINNED by a test asserting `"zh"` for a comment-only CJK document, annotated *"known limitation … Documenting behavior, not a bug"*. It was a bug. Assertion flipped, 5 regression cases added. Safe to land alone: `detect_language`'s only callers are tests and `run_all_for_language` (itself test-only), so **zero production behaviour change**. ⚠ **DO NOT seat the filter yet.** Two required contexts break — `smoke-cli` (57 producer-coverage variants across 17 rules; the earlier "21 rules" was wrong) and `unit-tests` (76+ sites). ⚠⚠ **The standard repair path silently DISARMS the gate**: `gen_producer_triggers.py` re-derives `expected_hex` from the current binary, so running it after the change rewrites all 57 goldens to "no change", deleting real coverage from 14 producers with no kill-test to notice. ⚠ **The detector fix is PARTIAL — three defects remain.** `has_katakana` covers only U+3080-30FF so it cannot see half-width katakana U+FF65-FF9F, **which is literally what `JA-001` is about**; `has_hangul` covers only Hangul Syllables U+AC00-D7AF so it cannot see Hangul Jamo U+1100-11FF, **which is literally what `KO-001` is about**; and there is no Cyrillic/Greek/Hebrew/Devanagari/Thai heuristic at all (that gap alone explains 5 of the 14 detector-too-weak flips). The remaining 44 need re-deciding on the SCRIPT-vs-CONVENTION discriminator. Order: (1) finish the tag audit on that basis — `CJK-001`/`CJK-002` normalise fullwidth punctuation in an ASCII context and are language-INDEPENDENT, so their `["zh";"ja";"ko"]` tag is wrong; (2) re-measure; (3) only then seat the filter, never regenerating triggers to go green.ackage[...french...]`, no `\setdefaultlanguage{french}`) — yet `LANG-003`, tagged `fr`, fires on **39** of them. `RU-001` (tagged `ru`) likewise fires on 6 of the 11 i18n golden fixtures and is forbidden in none of their `forbid:` lists. This is the largest user-visible defect found in Phase G and sits squarely on the project's stated style-rule goal. | **VERIFIED 2026-09-05** — 46 records enumerated by file, call graph traced, 939-paper sweep | M |
| OPEN-062 | OVERREJ | **`LANG-003` has two real logic bugs — and fixing BOTH does not help, because its premise is near-universal in LaTeX.** ⚠ **This row supersedes two earlier diagnoses of mine; the fix was built, measured, and REVERTED.** (1) The predicates OVERLAP: `fr_style = contains " ;" || contains " :"` and `en_style = contains ";\n" || contains ": "`, so the single substring `" : "` satisfies both and `has_both` is true on its own. Verified: `Theorem 1 : the result holds.` — plain English, no math, no code — FIRES, while `Voici :` (real French) does not. (2) `verbatim` is never blanked, so LMFDB-style snippets `{'label' : {'$in': ...}}` supply both halves. ⚠ **But a rewrite fixing BOTH (disjoint prev-char classification + verbatim blanking, all three real French fixtures still firing, both reproducers silenced) moved corpus firing 183 → 187 of 287 papers — measured twice, reproducible, NOT a race.** The reason is the finding that matters: **65% of LaTeX papers contain both a spaced and an unspaced colon**, so "uses both conventions" is a property of ordinary LaTeX source, not evidence of mixing. The rule is therefore **not repairable by predicate-tuning** — it needs retirement, or a fundamentally stronger predicate (document actually French, plus an inconsistency ratio rather than mere co-occurrence). **What actually suppresses it is the language gate** (OPEN-061): ~0 papers declare French, so gating takes it 248 → 1. ⚠ Do not let that hide the logic bugs — they are real and would misfire on a genuinely French document with a code block. Bounded throughout: `Info` severity, no language-tagged rule has `produces_fix=true`, and ZERO overlap with `compile_blocking_ids`, so this can never manufacture a false-READY. | **VERIFIED 2026-09-06** — predicate overlap proved by construction; rewrite built and swept twice on the same 287-paper sample (183 → 187), then reverted | S |
| OPEN-063 | INSTR | **The language-tag audit was asked the wrong question — TWICE, in opposite directions — and only 2 of 46 tags can be decided without it.** A first pass classified tags by "does the rule's predicate already require the tagged language's bytes?" and named 10 mis-tags; a full 46-rule audit found 12, **a different 12** — it rated `HE-001`, `KO-001`, `FR-008`, `PT-001`, `DE-006` LOAD-BEARING where the first pass had called them mis-tagged, so acting on the first list would have deleted 5 tags that suppress real cross-language false positives. I then ran a refutation pass asking "can it fire on a document NOT in the tagged language?" — and **that test is also wrong**: it scored `EL-001`/`HI-001`/`TH-001` KEEP because they fire on English documents, when firing there is precisely the POINT (Greek script carries Ancient Greek, Devanagari carries Sanskrit, Thai carries Pali, and all appear inside English classics/Indology/NLP papers). ⚠ **The correct discriminator is SCRIPT vs CONVENTION**: a rule keyed on foreign SCRIPT bytes is correct wherever that script appears, so its language tag is pure loss (`CY-001` Cyrillic initials, `EL-001` Greek oxia/tonos); a rule keyed on a language-general pattern that is merely a CONVENTION of language X is wrong outside X, so its tag is load-bearing (`LANG-003` spaced colons). "Fires cross-language" does not discriminate — both kinds do. ⚠ Until each of the remaining 44 tags is re-decided on that basis, **untagging is deferred**; only `CJK-001`/`CJK-002` were changed, because their predicate requires `is_ascii_context` and so contradicts a `[zh;ja;ko]` tag under ANY reading. | **VERIFIED 2026-09-06** — 46 rules audited across 4 agents, 9 re-attacked by a refutation pass, contradictions traced to the test specification | M |
| OPEN-064 | SOUND | ✅ **PARTLY RESOLVED 2026-09-07 — the two document-corrupting rule defects are fixed; `TH-001` noise remains.** **`HI-001` — fix producer WITHDRAWN.** It deleted the joiner following a Devanagari virama, but VIRAMA+ZWJ and VIRAMA+ZWNJ are not misuse: they are the devices The Unicode Standard (ch. 12.1) defines to control conjunct formation — VIRAMA+ZWJ requests the half-form that writes the Marathi/Nepali EYELASH RA, VIRAMA+ZWNJ the explicit halant of Sanskrit citation forms. Measured harm: 146 bytes → 143 on an English paper quoting Marathi. `produces_fix` is now `false` with a documented reason, the ledger carries a new **WITHDRAWN** status (distinct from "pending", which would imply a producer is still owed), and the three tests that PINNED the deletion are replaced by their inverses, including one asserting the eyelash-ra is byte-identical after `--apply-fixes`. ⚠ **The predicate is deliberately NOT inverted.** The genuinely inert case is the mirror image — a joiner NOT adjacent to a virama — but this corpus has ZERO incidence of any of these byte patterns, so a replacement could not be validated against real documents, and an unvalidated "correction" is exactly what made `LANG-003` worse and had to be reverted. The rule stays Info-severity noise until an Indology/Marathi corpus exists. **`EL-001` — THREE defects, all fixed.** (1) Its `oxia_map` mapped U+1FEB (CAPITAL UPSILON WITH OXIA) to **U+038B, which is UNASSIGNED in Unicode** — the fixer wrote a non-character into the author's Greek; correct target is U+038E. (2) The detector counted the whole U+1F00–1FFF polytonic block while the fixer knew only the map's 14 codepoints, so a correct polytonic text reported N defects and `--apply-fixes-for EL-001` returned it BYTE-IDENTICAL; the count is now exactly the set of edits the fixer can produce. That divergence was **deliberate** — a code comment justified it as keeping a release differential byte-identical — and it was wrong twice over, because psili/dasia/perispomeni are correct polytonic Greek with no tonos equivalent, and a 4×64-wide byte window fires on random binary by chance. (3) It scanned inside `filecontents*`, verbatim and comments; it now reads `blank_exempt s`. ⚠ **The pinned test and the golden fixture WERE the bug**: `test_validators_locale.ml` asserted EL-001 *fires* on U+1F00 psili, and `corpora/lint/locale/EL-001_oxia_tonos.tex` contained a psili and **zero oxia** — the golden that "proved" the rule worked contained no instance of the defect it names. Both are corrected. Verified end-to-end: the golden now reports count=1, rewrites U+1F71→U+03AC, leaves the psili untouched, 54→53 bytes. Neither rule is compile-blocking, so no verdict moved. **Still open:** `TH-001` fires on a Thai token on its own line in an English NLP paper (noise, no fix producer, no corruption). | **VERIFIED 2026-09-07** — `[locale] PASS 117 cases`, contracts+ledger regenerated, `check_regression_gates`/`check_proof_substance`/`check_verbatim_safety` green | M |
| OPEN-065 | SOUND | ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-09-07. `lead-byte-without-continuation` — THREE instances, all now fixed.** A byte scan tests a UTF-8 LEAD byte and acts without checking that the following bytes are continuations (`b land 0xC0 = 0x80`), so latin-1 text trips it. (1) **ENC-008** — latin-1 `î`/`ï` counted as private-use; 5 papers fired, 1 after (OPEN-059). (2) **The language detector** — all four of `has_cjk_codepoints`/`has_katakana`/`has_hangul`/`has_arabic`; latin-1 `é` read as Chinese (OPEN-061). (3) **CJK-010** — `is_cjk_byte0 b = b >= 0xE4 && b <= 0xE9` at both adjacency sites; measured **38 papers fired before, 35 after**, and the three removed (`2506.15641v1`, `2507.00531v1`, `2507.08692v1`) are the SAME latin-1 files ENC-008's guard cleared — one set of documents was tripping several lead-byte scanners at once. ⚠ **CORRECTION to my own earlier entry: EL-001 is NOT a member of this class.** Its `b1 ∈ [0xBC,0xBF]` and `b2 ∈ [0x80,0xBF]` tests ARE valid continuation ranges, so the sequence it matches is well-formed UTF-8 for U+1F00-1FFF. It fires on a 60 KB random blob because such a payload contains that valid sequence by chance (~1.5e-5 per offset) and the rule scans inside `filecontents*` — that is NON-TEXT-REGION blindness, tracked with the other rule defects in OPEN-064, not here. I had lumped them together. ⚠ The class is closed but the SHAPE remains worth gating: a lead-byte test not followed by a continuation check is mechanically detectable in source, and three instances surfaced within two days. | **VERIFIED 2026-09-07** — all three fixed and regression-tested; CJK-010 swept over the full corpus before/after | M |
| OPEN-066 | SOUND | ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-09-07 — the whole class, plus the gate that could not see it.** `--apply-fixes` injected `\usepackage{xeCJK}` from CJK inside a `%` comment; xeCJK aborts under pdflatex, so a compiling paper became one pdflatex refuses to build. Reproduced at the pin (**rc 0 → inject → rc 1**), incidence **10 of 191 real roots (5.2%)**, all ten comment/verbatim-only. ⚠ **The required gates were BLIND, and I proved it by putting the bug back**: with the corruption live in the binary, `check_verbatim_safety` and `check_apply_fixes_safety` both exit **0**. `check_verbatim_safety` asks *"were the bytes INSIDE the region preserved?"* — the v27.1.4 class of in-place character rewrites. This defect never touches the region: it READS evidence there and writes to the PREAMBLE, so the region is byte-identical and the gate passes. ✅ **New arm `check_no_outside_edit`**: plants the battery in one region at a time and asserts everything OUTSIDE it is byte-identical. ⚠ The battery also had to be widened — it carried CJK PUNCTUATION but **no Han ideograph**, which is what CJK-004 actually keys on, so the family was untestable until that byte was added; `\ruby{`, `\toprule`, `\enquote{`, `\autoref` joined it. ✅ **All five `mk_usepackage_insert` rules fixed** — CJK-004, CJK-006, PKG-011, PKG-012, REF-011 — by detecting on `blank_exempt s` (new, length-preserving) while inserting at ORIGINAL offsets, the split REF-011 already documented. All five verified to still fire on live evidence. ⚠ **Scope stated honestly**: the arm covers author-verbatim + comment + url only. The three ARGUMENT regions (`\input{}`, `\usepackage{}`, `\label{}`) are excluded on a judgement about TeX, not convenience — those bytes ARE processed, and measured, a trigger inside `\label{}` injects all five, which for hyperref is defensible since `\autoref` there really does expand and really is undefined without it. Needs (a) a gate asserting each generated `*_chk` agrees with its OCaml rule on a differential battery, or (b) removal of the affected ids from the `*_proved` catalogues. | **VERIFIED 2026-09-07** — divergence read off the three sources directly | M |

The gate gained a `DEAD_REGIONS` arm and was KILL-TESTED — neutering the scanner produces 10 violations, each showing all five packages injected into the preamble from a dead span. Five dead-span fixtures were added to `corpora/apply_fixes/` so the pdflatex oracle covers the class too; it previously contained NOT ONE document with `\iffalse`, `\endinput`, `filecontents` or content after `\end{document}`, so oracle property (b) was vacuous here. **Still open:** the fixer also REWRITES prose inside an `\iffalse` span (`---` → en-dash, `...` → `\dots`) while a `%` comment is byte-preserved — two opposite policies for bytes that are equally dead. Failure direction is stated at the definition — denying wrongly loses a cosmetic fix, allowing wrongly ships a document pdflatex refuses to build. Three overclaims in D1 (`PdflatexFatalChannels.v`), my own file, found by an adversarial read.** **(1)** The header and the `check_print_assumptions` capstone entry both said the three channels mean **"nothing else a DOCUMENT contains"** can make the model refuse. False as written: what a document contains reaches the model only through the OCaml encoder, which is outside Coq, so a feature the encoder never turns into a token or an edge is invisible to the theorem. Both restated to "the model's image of a project". **(2)** §2 framed a MODEL DEFECT as an achievement — `acyclicity_is_not_a_fatal_channel` invites the reading "cycles do not matter", but its witness is a node with an edge to ITSELF (a `.tex` that `\input`s itself) and real pdflatex recurses until `TeX capacity exceeded`. The separation is genuine AND it is a place the model is WEAKER than the engine: it certifies a project the engine refuses. Now stated that way, with the honest conclusion — closing the gap means giving cycles a fatal channel, not deleting T2. **(3)** `ch_decl`'s project parameter was unused — "a channel of the project that is not a function of the project"; kept for uniform shape, now named `_p` with the reason recorded. ⚠ **Not fixed, and it is the real limitation: two of the three channels cannot fire on any input the shipped tool can build** — `declared_features` is hard-wired `[]` so Channel 2 is unreachable, and Channel 1 (T2) fired once historically. The recommended next proof is therefore **D2a, `BuildGraphFrontEnd.v`, making Channel 1 reachable** rather than another model-internal theorem. | **VERIFIED 2026-09-07** — proofs rebuild clean after all three edits; 6 capstones Closed | S |

| OPEN-090 | SOUND | ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-09-12 — `mk_usepackage_insert` now finds its anchor in the COMMENT-BLANKED view, with a control-word boundary.** `blank_nonlive` is length- and newline-preserving, so the offset still indexes the real bytes; if no LIVE `\documentclass` is found the function returns no edit, i.e. the diagnostic fires and nothing is inserted — the state that shipped for years. Verified on `2506.14914v1`: `\usepackage{booktabs}` now lands at line 37, immediately after the live class at line 36, instead of line 26 among the commented-out ones. Pinned by a `mutate` variant carrying the commented-class shape. ⚠ The adversarial reviewer MEASURED that the claim "this change is subtractive" is FALSE — across 2,961 corpus papers, 61 files trigger PKG-011 and 35 emit an edit today, so the anchor change moves behaviour on more files than the one it fixes; that population is recorded and unaudited. ⚠ The VIRGIN window shows two `! LaTeX Error: Missing \begin{document}.` breaks (OPEN-094) which may be the same insertion family in a new shape. Original: **The OPEN-066 package-injection class is REOPENED by a different producer and a different mechanism: `PKG-011` inserts `\usepackage{booktabs}` after a COMMENTED-OUT `\documentclass`.** Found by `check_apply_fixes_real_differential` on its first run and then reproduced by hand. `2506.14914v1` (Elsevier `elsarticle` template) compiles rc 0 at the pin; `--apply-fixes` adds `\usepackage{booktabs}` after line 25 — and lines 20-33 of that template are a block of commented-out alternative `\documentclass` lines, with the LIVE `\documentclass[times,twocolumn,final]{elsarticle}` at **line 36**. The package therefore lands ten lines before the document class and pdflatex dies `! LaTeX Error: \usepackage before \documentclass.` ⚠ **Two known classes compose here, which is why neither gate saw it**: the producer chooses its insertion point with a `\documentclass` scan that is COMMENT-BLIND (trap #1, C-27), and the fix INTRODUCES a package dependency (the CJK-004 / TYPO-038 inverse, OPEN-066/075). OPEN-066 is marked RESOLVED for "the whole class"; that is now too strong — it fixed reading a TRIGGER out of dead bytes, while this is choosing a WRITE POSITION from dead bytes. The remedy is the same shape as OPEN-066's gate but on the other side of the edit: an insertion point must be validated against the comment-blanked view, and a producer that emits a preamble line must prove the preamble it found is live. | **VERIFIED 2026-09-12** — measured by the new differential, then reproduced by hand: `\documentclass` located at :36, injection at :26, rc 0 before and rc 1 after at the pin | M |

It is now written as an explicit pair (attempt 1 `continue-on-error` + a partial-switch discard + an unguarded attempt 2), because a `uses:` step cannot be wrapped in a shell loop. **(2) The retry loop that DID exist could not fail.** `for attempt in 1 2 3; do probe && break; sleep 15; done` exits 0 when every attempt fails — under `bash -eo pipefail` a command failing inside an `&&` list does not trip `-e`, and the loop's status is that of its last command, `sleep`. A sweep of every `for`/`while` in `.github/` found **four live instances, all readiness waits**: setup-ocaml-env's dependency install (reached by **28 workflows**) and the warmup loops in `rust-proxy-smoke`, `rest-smoke`, `rest-schema`. **Measured, not inferred: extracted from `origin/main`, probe stubbed to always fail, all four exited 0; after the fix all four exit 1, and still exit 0 when the probe succeeds.** ⚠ **This is a live finding about OPEN-056, not just a tidy-up**: the readiness PRECONDITION that the whole `rust-proxy-smoke` diagnosis assumed was never enforced — 45 dead warmups let the smoke proceed against workers that had not answered once. It does not overturn C-53 (the handshake still measured worse), but it does mean the baseline was taken with the precondition unchecked. Gated by failure modes 4 and 5 of `check_workflow_triggers.py` (required `spec-drift`), which now scans composite actions too — previously invisible to it, and where the worst instance lived. | OPEN-101 | GATE | ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-09-20, and the binary arm was further DEMOTED 2026-09-24 (C-68): it false-fired on a comment-only edit, so it is now a failure only when the engine source is UNCHANGED — the stale-build case, which measurement showed is the one case it alone can see.** — but NOT the way this row first said, and the wrong version is kept below because the mistake is the useful part (C-64).** A platform-independent engine anchor now travels with every position artefact: `src_tree_sha`, git's own tree id for `latex-parse/src`, recorded by all three producers (`diff_real_roots.py`, `gen_proven_coverage.py`, `gen_apply_fixes_real_differential.py`) and checked by both gates through the shared choke point. It is EXACT where the commit count is a proxy, and it is sharper in the useful direction: a tree id is unchanged by any commit that does not touch the engine, so an artefact measured many commits ago can be proved CURRENT rather than merely within tolerance — the proxy can only bound staleness. Backfilled onto all five artefacts with ZERO re-measurement, deriving each from the sha it already recorded; all five resolve to `ee1d71dd` = HEAD's engine tree, so all five are now provably current. Optional by construction: absent, the distance ratchet applies exactly as before, so it can only make the gate smarter, never weaker. Kill-test registered (`engine source moved under the measurement`) and verified in both directions. `cli_sha256` stays as the machine-local check it actually is, with its skip announced. ORIGINAL ROW FOLLOWS. **The strongest arm `check_project_state.py` owns — the one its own comment calls *the fact*, as against the commit-count *proxy* — executes in NO CI job.** The comment reads *"When the CLI is built (the `build` job; spec-drift is a pure job and has none)"*, which states a fallback that does not exist: an exhaustive sweep on 2026-09-20 (`grep -rn check_project_state` across workflows, Makefile, shell and python) found exactly ONE invocation, `.github/workflows/spec-drift.yml`, a job that compiles nothing. So `cli_hash` is always `None` in CI and the `cli_sha256` comparison has never run there — on any PR, ever. It fires only on a developer laptop that happens to have a built binary. Partially mitigated in this PR: the skip is now ANNOUNCED on stderr instead of taken silently, because a check nobody can see not running is indistinguishable from one that passed. ⚠ **CORRECTED 2026-09-20, same day, by me: the fix I wrote in this row is IMPOSSIBLE.** It read *"invoke `check_project_state.py` from the `build` job in `ci.yml`, where the CLI exists"*. That cannot work. `ci.yml:15` runs on `ubuntu-22.04` and `file` reports the artefact-producing binary as `Mach-O 64-bit executable arm64` — a Linux x86-64 ELF and a macOS arm64 Mach-O can never share a sha256. And the artefacts CANNOT be regenerated in CI either: the corpus is 12 GB of non-redistributable arXiv source that is deliberately not in the repo. So **`cli_sha256` is a MACHINE-LOCAL check whose enforcement venue is wrong, not a check someone forgot to wire** — every artefact is produced on a maintainer's machine and verified against a binary only that machine can build. Wiring it as written would have turned a silent skip into a permanent red. (I found this by testing my own row's premise before acting on it: first `strings` for embedded paths, which came back clean and nearly talked me out of it, then `file` on the binary, which settled it.) **THE ACTUAL FIX, and it is a strengthening rather than a wiring job:** record a PLATFORM-INDEPENDENT source anchor next to the binary one — `git rev-parse <measured_at_sha>:latex-parse/src`, the tree hash of the engine source at measurement time — and check THAT in `spec-drift`, which already has `fetch-depth: 0` after C-58. It is exact where the commit-distance proxy is approximate (it catches an uncommitted engine edit, and is immune to commit-count churn that does not touch the tree), it needs no binary and no corpus, and it can be BACKFILLED onto the existing artefacts with no re-measurement at all, because the tree hash is recoverable from the sha each one already records. Keep `cli_sha256` as the local-run check it actually is, and keep announcing the skip. | MEASURED 2026-09-20 — exhaustive grep for every invocation; `cli_hash` provably `None` in the only job that runs it | S |

Discriminating test: point the backup directory outside the synced tree and see whether leftovers stop. ⚠ **NOT EVERY RUN: on 2026-09-25 a full pure + binary run left `.gate-selftest-backups/` EMPTY, and the 2026-09-24 leftovers were cleared only after `cmp` showed all four byte-identical to their live files. So the defect is intermittent, which makes it harder to see, not easier.** **`check_gate_selftests` prints its "a backup was not consumed — inspect before trusting the tree" WARNING on SUCCESSFUL runs, so it fires every time and therefore warns about nothing.** Observed 2026-09-24 on a fully green run (pure 41/24, binary 3/2, RC=0 both levels): five files left in `.gate-selftest-backups/`, all timestamped within that run, all byte-identical to their live counterparts, working tree clean. The warning is correct that backups remain and wrong that it means anything — and a genuinely stranded backup, which is what it exists to surface, would now be invisible behind the noise. Same shape as the rest of this week's findings: a signal that cannot distinguish its alarm state from its normal state. FIX: consume (or delete) each backup once its mutation is restored and verified, and warn only on what remains. ⚠ Do NOT just silence it — the condition it names is real, the reporting is what is broken. It does NOT yet license an allow-list: silent damage was measured only for implicated rules, so a rule with zero breaks is not thereby shown meaning-preserving. `preserved` means rc 0, and this row proves that is not the same as `unchanged in meaning`. THE REVIEW (`corpora/apply_fixes_real/fix_meaning_review.json`): 10 classifier agents judged every changed word (rebuilding past the 25-hunk cap), then every SAFE verdict faced an independent refuter searching the whole corpus and constructed edge cases. The unsafe verdicts are MEASURED, not precautionary, and they sort into classes: (a) **meaning changes in compiling papers** — SCRIPT-021 reorders `tensor`-package indices (`\tensor{R}{_a^b_c^d}` typesets as a different tensor; it edits 73% of papers), MATH-061 turns `\log_210` (log base 2 of 10) into `\log_{210}`, SCRIPT-001 turns the coordinates `a_10001` into `a_{10001}`, SCRIPT-005 renames the exponent `l` to `\ell` while its bound index stays `l`, TYPO-032 deletes 75 of 75 required commas between author-year citations, SPC-020 merges words after a `\$`, TYPO-042/TYPO-027 change `\texttt{!!x}` to `!x`; (b) **wrong for context at every site** — MATH-046 applies the reverse of the TeXbook ellipsis convention to all 496 of its edits, STYLE-033 deletes the space before `\cite` instead of inserting a tie (`in[12]`, 178 of 210), MATH-082 collapses deliberate `\!` kerning runs of up to 17 (2,306 edits), SPC-028 collapses deliberate `~~~` indentation (~97% of edits); (c) **cross-language** — RO-001 replaces correct Turkish `ş` with Romanian `ș` throughout a Turkish abstract, and PL-001 (Polish) fires on English papers: the fix channel inherits OPEN-061's unseated language gate; (d) **compile breaks the break sweep never saw** — PKG-011/REF-011 insert packages that the class or a local `.sty` already provides (option clash, `\toprule` already defined), MATH-015 emits `\overset` without amsmath, SPC-009 leaves `\\` opening a paragraph (real paper 2507.05913v1, rc 0→1); (e) **the custom-verbatim hole (OPEN-113)** — ten zero-diff hygiene rules (TYPO-018, SPC-008, STRUCT-003, SPC-031, STYLE-015, SPC-010, SPC-002, TYPO-017, MATH-053, SCRIPT-018) rewrite code inside author-defined listing environments. THE GATE: `check_fix_allowlist.py` (spec-drift) refuses any default rule that is implicated in a measured break, lacks a SAFE review with a refutation that found no damage, or lacks a compiling standalone audit build; three kill-tests registered. Every fixer gate (`check_apply_fixes_safety`, `check_verbatim_safety`, `check_apply_fixes_roundtrip`) now grades BOTH the default path and `-all`, because under convergence the allow-list run is not a subset of the full run. ⚠ **What this means for the product: auto-fixing by byte rewrite, as these rules implement it, is net-negative wherever a reader can see the result.** Every fix that visibly changed a PDF was measured wrong somewhere; the safe ones are the ones nobody can see. The detection value of all 50 rules is untouched — only their auto-apply moved behind an explicit opt-in. **CONFIRMED ON THE SEALED WINDOW** (frame ranks 2600-2718, held unused for exactly this; `scripts/tools/confirm_fix_policy.py`, artefact `corpora/apply_fixes_real/policy_confirmation_2600.json`; pristine control, pinned engine): of 104 papers that compile pristine and could be measured, **`--apply-fixes-all` (the old default) broke 13 = 12.5% and changed the typeset TEXT of 84 of the 91 it left compiling; `--apply-fixes` (the allow-list) broke 0 and changed the text of 0**, editing one paper (MATH-106, 12 edits, text identical). | OPEN-114 | SOUND | ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-09-26 — the L0 linter never terminated on a real paper, and the cause was ONE missing index advance in CY-001 (Cyrillic initials).** A plain lint of `2506.17069v1/PB.tex` (50 KB) ran more than 10 minutes at 100% CPU; `--layer l0` alone did not finish in 60 s while l1-l4 each finished in at most 0.2 s. Found by the OPEN-112 sealed-window confirmation, which refused to score the paper rather than timing out into a verdict (C-70). ROOT CAUSE (MEASURED): in `r_cy_001` (`validators_l0.ml`), after a Cyrillic capital followed by `. `, the only statement advancing the scan index sat inside `if n0 = 0xd0 && !i + 5 < len then (...)` with no else branch, so a capital, `. `, and any byte other than a Cyrillic lead byte (or a lead byte at end of input) left the loop spinning forever. A 5-byte input reproduces it (`А. \`); in the paper it is a Russian bibliography COMMENT, `% Неретин Ю. А. {\it ...` — comments do not protect, because the rule scans raw bytes. `sample` on the stuck process put 2,282 of 2,282 samples in that function. It is a true hang, not super-linear: 5 B to 100 KB inputs all failed to finish. FIX: the advance moved outside the guard, so every path through the dot-space branch moves forward by 4 bytes. Skipping them cannot lose a match (`.` and space cannot begin a Cyrillic capital), and the reviewer proved exactly that output can change only on inputs that used to hang. Evidence: plain-lint output byte-identical on 83/83 and 45/45 real files (the second set includes all 19 other Cyrillic files in the corpus); a regression test that forks a child with a 5 s kill FAILS 6 cases on the old code and passes on the new; a fuzzer over 95 multilingual fragments found 4 hangs in 600 inputs on the old binary and 0 in 33,000 on the new; a static read of all 23 same-shape loop guards in `validators_l0.ml`, `validators_l0_typo.ml` and `validators_common.ml` found no other instance. PB.tex now lints in 0.39 s. INCIDENCE (C-66): an L0 timing census of 8,602 files in 2,844 corpus papers found exactly ONE paper with the hang — the regex for the trigger matches that one file and no other. Small in reach, total in effect: a linter that never returns is a hard failure for the keystroke-latency goal, and any editor integration would have frozen on that paper. | MEASURED 2026-09-26 — minimisation, profiler samples, per-size timings, equivalence 128 files, fuzzing, corpus census | S |

Six files take 2-14 s in L0 and 8-45 s for a full lint — `2507.11536v1/UQIC.tex` (1.4 MB) 14 s, `2507.08909v1/APE4b.tex` (619 KB) 7 s, `2506.12601v1/reconstruction.tex` (914 KB) 6-8 s. They cost 7-13 µs per byte, close to the corpus median of 6.7 µs/B, so this is linear cost on large inputs, not a hang, and the CY-001 fix neither causes nor fixes it. Relevant to Track R (OPEN-104): a keystroke budget cannot be met by re-linting a whole megabyte file; the answer is incremental re-validation, not a faster full pass. ⚠ Timings were taken under heavy load and are pessimistic in absolute terms; the per-byte ratio is the robust number. | MEASURED 2026-09-26 — corpus-wide L0 timing census with a confirming re-run for every file over 5 s | M |

FIX, a whitelist instead of a blacklist: a run's rc counts only with POSITIVE PROOF that pdfTeX produced it — the container runs pdflatex under `sh -c`, which reports its rc on a per-run nonce line (the docker client's rc is ignored; no line = `OracleError`), and every graded run must carry pdfTeX's banner on stdout (native backend too); the shim maps EVERY exception to `INFRA_RC`; `pdflatex_ok` returns None (not graded) on `OracleError`; `false_ready_oracle.sh` checks each pass's banner (else `NOPROOF`) and the halt run's rc and log BEFORE deleting them; `diff_compile_check.sh` requires the log's banner. Post-fix, same wrapper: shim 125; `run_to_fixpoint` raises `OracleError`; `pdflatex_ok` None; `false_ready_oracle.sh` halt-only loss → 85 of 85 refused, exit 2; daemon lost after the 40th pdflatex → 10 graded `ok`, 75 refused, exit 2; `diff_compile_check.sh` lost after the 20th → 0 NEW rows, 45 not graded, exit 2. Healthy container: `false_ready_oracle.sh` 85/85, hard 0, soft 0, exit 0; the three apply_fixes_real windows re-run 0 of 120 cells, every row field identical. NEW GATE `check_oracle_infra_grading.py` (spec-drift, pure, 7 kill-tests in round 2, 14 after round 3) drives the real graders with fake docker/engine binaries in each no-proof shape (dead socket, daemon error, cut stream, no banner) and a genuine pdfTeX failure. It exercises the Python graders end to end but only the `run_pdflatex`/`drift_class` FUNCTIONS of `false_ready_oracle.sh` plus the order of its halt-run checks; the whole-script dead-daemon behaviour above is a local measurement (it needs docker), not a CI gate. I can't write on file `t.log'.`, `!pdfTeX error: pdflatex (file t.pdf): fwrite() failed`, `No space left on device`) and exits 1 — banner present, in-container rc line present, graded FAILS. FIX, three layers: (1) the graded ARGV is an ALLOW-LIST in `run_pdflatex`, the one entry point of `run_once`, `run_to_fixpoint` and the shim (`_oracle.check_engine_argv`): `-interaction=<mode>`, `-halt-on-error`, `-file-line-error`, `-recorder`, `-draftmode` (also with `--`) and exactly ONE file argument that does not start (after blanks) with `-`, `&`, `\` or `*`, holds no whitespace or control character (web2c joins argv into TeX's first line), has no `..` component, and is absolute only inside the run directory; everything else (`-cnf-line`, every shell-escape switch, `-output-directory`, `-jobname` — no grader passes it, grep — `-ini`, `-fmt`/`-progname`/`&fmt`, `-translate-file`, `-kpathsea-debug`, `-mktex*`, ...) is `OracleError` (the shim: `INFRA_RC` 125), never a grade; `run_engine` (gen_contract.py, not a grader) has its own allow-list (the graded flags plus `-ini`, `-etex`, `-jobname=[A-Za-z0-9_-]+`, `-progname=pdflatex`, `-translate-file=cp227.tcx`, one argument); (2) EVERY writable tree of kpathsea's `TEXMF` is private per run: `private_texmf_vars` adds `TEXMFCONFIG`, and `graded_env` requires all three (MEASURED in the pinned image, `kpsewhich -var-value=TEXMF` with `HOME=/tmp`: `{TEXMFCONFIG,TEXMFVAR,TEXMFHOME,!!TEXMFLOCAL,!!TEXMFSYSCONFIG,!!TEXMFSYSVAR,!!TEXMFDIST}`, the four `!!` trees belong to the image; with the private three set, `kpsewhich rvwpoison.sty` finds nothing while the planted file is present); (3) the CONTAINER must still be the image: `ContainerOracle.check_texmf_trees`, on every construction (the shim's too, one exec), refuses a container whose own default `TEXMFHOME`/`TEXMFVAR`/`TEXMFCONFIG` (as its `kpsewhich` reports them) hold any file; `ContainerOracle.check_state`, once per grading session (`get_oracle()` for the Python graders; `_oracle.py version`, which `oracle_setup` runs once per shell grader, and `info`), refuses a container any path of whose root filesystem has a CTIME after the container's creation, apart from a fixed allow-list (the mount points of the work root, `/tmp`, `/tmp/.texlive2026` and its empty directories, docker's `/etc/hosts`-type files, fontconfig's cache files by name); ctime, because a writer can set mtime back (`touch -d`) but not ctime. `docker diff` was MEASURED at 13-16 s under load, too slow even per session; the ctime scan (`find / -xdev -newerct`) at about 6 s. (o) THE DOCUMENT CANNOT WRITE THE ORACLE'S EVIDENCE — CLOSED 2026-09-30 (C-99 amended; review round 3 of fix/v27165-oracle-tmpdir @ 6371cc54). H1 (HIGH, MEASURED by the review, on main too): under `openout_any=p` a document `\immediate\openout`s its OWN `\jobname.log` while pdfTeX holds it, writes past the log's real end, ends it with a forged `Output written on \jobname.pdf (1 page, …)` and `\openout`s a 13-byte `\jobname.pdf`; `(n)`'s reader graded compiles although the same run's terminal ended with pdfTeX's genuine `No pages of output.`. FIX (by METHOD, three parts): (1) every engine run is SUPERVISED (`_oracle._SUPERVISOR_SRC`, native and container): a small process starts the engine with stdin `/dev/null` and counts, with inotify on the run directory, the close-writes of `<job>.log/.pdf/.fls/.fmt`; pdfTeX closes each once, so a second close-write (the document writing it, by name or through a symlink) REFUSES the run (OracleError, never a grade); no inotify or a queue overflow is refused too. The watch includes IN_MODIFY: the kernel MERGES an event identical to the unread tail event (MEASURED: `echo x > t.log; echo y >> t.log` counted ONE close-write under an IN_CLOSE_WRITE-only watch), and pdfTeX's final report (an IN_MODIFY) always separates the document's close-write from pdfTeX's own. (2) `pdf_written` reads pdfTeX's final report from the LOG and the TERMINAL (`EngineRun.stdout`), which must agree; text after a report is refused; a `\batchmode` document (silent terminal) is decided by the supervised log. The three pass-protocol copies all take it: `run_pass`/`run_to_fixpoint` (OracleRun.pdf), the shell graders (`oracle_pdf_written DIR FILE STDOUT`, exit 125 = not a grade), gen_contract `Tex.pdflatex` (its stdout RETURNED, not kept on the shared `Tex` object — the uncommitted round-3 draft kept it there, a cross-thread race in a multi-threaded generator). Package pdftex.def Error: File `x-eps-converted-to.pdf' not found`` before a genuine error IS the log's first `!` line; a `\message`d `l.99` likewise) and which `\PackageError` raises for real; `ungraded-infra` is RETIRED and a cell is `cell_of(compiles, ready)`; the first-error block is a diagnostic read by `_oracle.first_error_block`. | OPEN-125 | GATE | ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-10-02 (branch `fix/v27165-pool-drop`) — THE MULTI-WORKER POOL DROPPED REQUESTS, AND IT WAS NOT THE CONTAINER: FOUR BROKER DEFECTS, EACH NOW A FAILING-BEFORE TEST.** (This is the row #631 added on `main` as the open item from #628. It was briefly numbered OPEN-127 on this branch to avoid an id collision, and is merged back under its `main` id.) Symptom (#628, linux/arm64 under colima): with `L0_POOL_CORES=0,1`, 2 of 6 REST and 3 of 6 direct UDS requests unanswered; one worker 6 of 6; the image was switched to one worker. **Reproduced natively on macOS arm64, no container**: a fixed-content UDS probe (one connection per request, 5 s timeout) answered 7 of 12 with two workers and 12 of 12 with one. **Mechanism, each step observed, not inferred**: (1) a units bug in `worker.ml` (`/. 1.048_576` where 1,048,576 was meant) made every reply report about 10^6 times its allocation (14,251.7 MB for a 64-byte request; raw words 1,868), so the broker marked the worker Cooling and RETIRED IT AFTER EVERY REQUEST (19 rotations in 20 requests); (2) workers are forked and never exec, so `set_close_on_exec` closed nothing and every worker inherited the parent's end of every EARLIER worker's socketpair (`lsof`: worker 2 held fd 5 = the parent end of worker 1's pair, both held the listening socket); retiring worker 1 closes the parent's end and `waitpid`s for an EOF that worker 2's copy withholds, so the request that triggered the retirement hung in `maybe_rotate` (`sample`: main_service blocked in `Broker.maybe_rotate`, worker 1 blocked in `read`) until a LATER request retired worker 2 — hence about one request in three. Two further defects surfaced once the hang was gone: (3) main_service runs one thread per connection and every thread drove the same pool, timer and sockets unserialised — 4 concurrent clients got 78 of 100 calls wrong or failed, including mis-decoded frames (`status=20 tokens=0`); (4) a hedge's loser reply was never read (`Cancel` cannot interrupt a single-threaded worker, and a 200 ms "timeout recovery" cleared `inflight` flags), so a later request read an earlier request's reply. **Fix** (`latex-parse/src/broker.ml`, `worker.ml`, `hedge_timer*`, `main_service.ml`; `core/l0_lexer` symlinks the same files): the child closes every inherited fd except stdio and its own (listing `/proc/self/fd` or `/dev/fd`; `init_pool` fails loudly if neither is listable); the units are fixed and the reply carries allocation SINCE SPAWN; `hedged_call` holds a pool mutex; a per-worker `outstanding` count replaces `inflight`, and a worker is reused only after the replies it owes are read; a dead worker is replaced at once (before, a Hot worker that died was never respawned); the kqueue stub deregisters its fds after each wait and deletes a stale timer before re-arming; EINTR is retried in OCaml, not C; a connection thread closes its socket on every exit. **Regression test** `test_broker_pool` (in `dune runtest latex-parse/src`; parent-enforced 90 s deadline per scenario): against `origin/main` both scenarios HANG; with only the units fix reverted, P1 fails (19 rotations); with only the fd fix reverted, P2 (`retire 0; retire 1; retire 0`) hangs; with only the mutex removed, P3 fails (78/100) and H2 (20/40); with the loser reply not drained, the hedge scenario fails; fixed: passes, 3 of 3 runs. **Same defect as OPEN-056** (its harness, 10 runs each, same machine): `origin/main` 23 attempt-failures and 1 failed run; fixed 0 and 0. `scripts/tools/docker_smoke.sh` now checks the default AND a two-worker pool: 10 consecutive 200s plus 4 clients x 5 concurrent. **REMAINING SCOPE**: (a) NOT re-measured in a linux/arm64 container — colima's docker disk was 99% full (1.1 GB free) and the image build needs more; the PR's `docker-push` run is the first container measurement (linux/amd64). (b) The image default stays at ONE worker until (a) is run; restoring `L0_POOL_CORES=0,1` is an owner decision.
