## 4. Corrections log — what we got wrong

The most valuable section. Each entry is a belief that was acted on and turned
out to be false, plus how it was caught, so the same mistake is not repeated.

| # | We believed | Actually | How it surfaced |
|---|---|---|---|
| C-1 | Driving the false-READY **fixture** baseline down was progress toward the North Star. | The corpora are **disjoint**. 21 → 7 across ~15 PRs moved the published banner **35 → 34**. | Running the first differential against real papers (#538). |
| C-2 | The residual false-READY set was **small and enumerable**. | 11/199 = 5.5% on real papers, and every instrument pointed at the problem has found more. | Same. |
| C-3 | The cardinal bug (false-READY) was the dominant failure mode. | **Over-rejection is far larger**: 37.7%, i.e. 92.6% of every NOT-READY verdict on a real paper is wrong. | Same. |
| C-4 | The T5 rule engine was catching compile failures. | On 200 real papers the compile-blocking set fired on 25 documents, **all 25 compile**, and caught **none** of the 6 real failures (T2 caught 5, T0 caught 1). ⚠ The corpus is papers arXiv *built*, so this shows miscalibration for the real population, **not** vacuity on broken input. | Recomputing from `results.json`. |
| C-5 | A verdict flip from NOT-READY to READY is a win. | Only if the oracle agrees. Of 22 flips from the DELIM fix, **20 compiled and 2 did not** — the bug had been masking two real false-READYs. | Recompiling all 22 before claiming the result. |
| C-6 | A fixture reproducing the right *error message* is the right fixture. | `fr_thmtools_redeclare` is **strong-fatal**; the wild-type shape is **error-halt**. A detector validated only on the synthetic one would miss every real paper. | Measuring both pinned protocols for both fixtures. |
| C-7 | Adding a corpus of lint fixtures was safe. | `corpora/unicode` (#533) was never graded against pdflatex; **6 of 14 are live false-READYs**. | Auditing my own work after the position assessment. |
| C-8 | "Carried" reason counts show which fix rescues most documents. | They rescue **nothing** — `T5` co-occurs with every T5 rule id by construction. Only **rescue-alone** (a document's *only* blocking reason) is actionable. | Recomputing; the first ordering was wrong. |
| C-9 | A green local checklist meant the change was verified. | Two runs reported "clean" that had been **killed before writing output**. Background jobs now write a `SENTINEL_DONE` marker. | A CI `format` failure on #535 that the local check had "passed". |
| C-10 | `check_roadmap_facts.py` passing meant the ROADMAP was true. | It asserts only the numbers it knows, via `re.search`, so of two contradictory matrices only the first is checked. It passed while ≥4 statements were false. | Reading the gate. |
| C-11 | Findings reported by an audit agent could be recorded and planned against as-is. | Of **six** such items, **two were wrong**. There is no second live confusion matrix in the ROADMAP (`:140` states the correct tuple and explains the superseded one), and `:84`'s "0 over-rejection" is a *requirement on new gates*, not a claim about the tool. A third, OPEN-004, was half wrong: relative `\input` does escape the project root, but absolute paths are correctly rejected. | Reproducing all six before planning. Items now carry VERIFIED-with-evidence or say UNVERIFIED. |
| C-12 | The `\lstinline` scanner defect was "a misplaced range exposing verbatim". | Narrower and doubled. Corruption needs a `[` inside the option VALUE (`\lstinline[caption={[x]}]\|a--b\|` → `a–b`). The common case is the opposite failure: the range runs to **EOF**, silently withholding every fix in the rest of the document. The second half was recorded nowhere. | Reproducing it instead of trusting the summary. |
| C-13 | A generated block should carry every fact worth knowing, including release debt. | `git describe` changes on **every commit**, so the regenerate-and-diff gate fired on the very next one. A gate that fires on every PR trains people to regenerate without reading, which is worse than no gate. Volatile facts belong in prose as an instruction, not in a diffed block. | The gate failing on my own next commit. |
| C-14 | OPEN-009's `\ref*` gap was "a one-character fix", cheap and safe. | **Strictly negative as scoped.** Adding `*` to the opt-skip makes the detector skip the span, and a document whose `\ref` is redefined in an `\input` child goes from correctly NOT-READY (pdflatex rc 1, no PDF) to READY. Measured yield of the "fix": **0 of 200** and **0 of 2,961**. So it trades a manufactured false-READY for nothing. | An adversarial agent tasked with REFUTING each "no false-READY risk" claim, then reproduced by hand. The ledger had been instructing the patch. |
| C-15 | The `\verb` scanner's only defect was the optional-argument delimiter. | There is a third: the delimiter is read at a FIXED offset, but TeX absorbs a space after a control word. `X \verb |ab| Y` compiles with `|` as delimiter while the scanner takes the space. A "fix" that re-implements the same offset rule in the re-key inherits the bug. | Same hunt. |
| C-16 | The generated-block gate meant the published position was correct. | It only proved the block matched its SOURCE. `results.json` went **three PRs stale** while the gate passed, publishing **56.8%** when main measured **65.8%**. A source-of-truth needs provenance on its sources, not just consistency with them. | Noticing the headline disagreed with a number I had measured an hour earlier. |
| C-32 | Having named a trap makes me immune to it (second offence). | OPEN-031 recorded '2507.10809v1 fails while only LOADING tabu, never using the environment' — measured with a scan that globbed `$paper/*.tex`, i.e. TOP-LEVEL ONLY. The paper has **5 tabu environments** in `sections/camera_ready/methods.tex`, live via `\input`. The include-closure trap is trap #2 in the standing agent briefing, cited in OPEN-024, and it had already produced six wrong 'no thmtools' verdicts in the OPEN-002 derivation — and I still shipped a ledger claim from a closure-blind scan. C-27 was the same failure with comments. **Scans that feed LEDGER CLAIMS need the same closure+comment hygiene as detectors**; a claim's evidence column should name the scan's scope so the blindness is visible. | The delta-debug agent reading the child files my glob skipped. |
| C-33 | 2026-09-01 | **The first draft of the ws-tolerant env matcher tolerated spaces on the CLOSE side too — wrong, and a manufacture vector.** TeX is ASYMMETRIC: `\begin {verbatim}` opens (control-word space absorption) but `\end {verbatim}` does NOT close (`\@xverbatim` matches the literal byte string). The symmetric version ended ranges early and exposed live verbatim bytes to comment blanking (measured: blanked `% 表` after a spaced end → the T3 signal vanished). Caught BEFORE ship by the C-30 adversarial pass, alongside 5 breaker-gate misses (`\Verb`, `\verbdef`, `\@makeother\%`, direct `tcblisting`, xparse `v`-spec), the root-only guard scope, and the single-newline `\begin` form — all 8 measured with pinned pdflatex reproducers, all fixed or pinned in the same PR. The verifier-first rule is not optional. |
| C-34 | 2026-09-02 | **"The gate sees the ROOT bytes only — structural here" was FALSE, and the root-only T2 gate was a measured false-READY.** A `%`-catcode breaker carried in an `\input` child or a local `.sty` changes the ROOT's later `%`-semantics: two constructed vectors (child `\catcode`\%=9` + root `%\input{missing}`; same via `\usepackage`-loaded local sty) are pdflatex-fatal yet were READY under the draft's root-only gate. The circularity claim dissolves because the COMMENT-BLIND candidate scan is a SUPERSET of the live edges — probing that superset is conservative and complete. Caught pre-ship by the C-30 design-scout pass; fixed with `collect_probe_sources` + the `fr_t2_child_catcode` tree fixture. Same lesson as C-33: the convenient scoping claim in a fresh comment is exactly what the adversarial pass exists to test. |
| C-35 | 2026-09-03 | **"Zero natural incidence" does not make a demotion safe — the blanket net-positive DELIM-001 demotion was REFUTED pre-ship.** The frame sweep found 0 brace-caused failures among 40 net-positive-never-negative papers, and I nearly read that as "demote unconditionally". The C-30 scout constructed three pdflatex-fatal vectors with the IDENTICAL byte signature (`\textbf{` runaway = strong-fatal with DELIM-001 as its ONLY blocker; unclosed math group; `\def`-body), found the THIRD enforcement point (PRT-001 via Partial_cst on both paths), and the fixture trap (fr_cmt_m2's surfaced error is byte-identical to the naive T0 filter's target). The shipped policy is position-aware (math/argument-position stay fatal; `}`-chaining distinguishes `\frac{a}{` from `{bare}{`), the parser message is TYPED (depth>0), and the exoneration carries a live-`\begin{document}` guard — each arm pinned by its own fr_delim_* fixture. Natural-incidence measurements bound the EXPECTED cost; constructible vectors bound the WORST CASE — a gate change needs both. |
| C-36 | 2026-09-03 | **The parser-policy draft manufactured two false-READYs the fixture battery could not see — both oracle-confirmed pre-ship by the C-30 pass.** R1: `\newcommand{\dead}{\iffalse}` hides the conditional inside the OPAQUE definition body (change A) so the tail-stop's lexical if-depth guard (change B) misses it and fires on a TeX-dead `\end{document}` — the changes COMPOSED against each other, and a natural relative sits in-sample (`2507.10709v1`'s `\def\iflong{\iffalse`). R2: the definition-argument %-comment skip ran inside EVERY command's brace args, so `\url{...%20...}` overshot its closing brace — one manufactured READY on an error-halt doc AND one over-rejection regression on its compiling twin. Fixes: raw definition bodies containing backslash-if PERMANENTLY poison the tail-stop (suppression = old behaviour); tex_aware argument semantics confined to the definition-signature branch. Lesson on top of C-35: when two independently-sound policies ship together, the adversarial pass must attack their COMPOSITION — each drafted change was safe alone. |
| C-37 | 2026-09-03 | **The closure walk's directive matcher was the gate's blind spot: braceless `\input name` children never reached the breaker probe.** The OPEN-042 draft was refuted by the C-30 pass with a measured manufactured false-READY in two spellings — a child carrying `%`-catcode surgery behind the PRIMITIVE `\input pctchild` form (present in 76/2,961 real papers) was invisible to `closure_segments` (it matched only `\input{`/`\include{`), so `comment_blanking_breakers` saw no breaker and the new ENC filter dropped a genuinely fatal fire. Fixed by resolving-and-splicing the braceless form in the closure walk; the UNRESOLVED case cannot manufacture because T2's missing-file check holds those documents (kpathsea tree files included). ⚠ Residual, recorded: the walk's fuel caps (48 files / depth 6) share the failure shape — a breaker in the 49th file or at depth 7 is unseen; no measured incidence, revisit if a closure ever hits the caps. The channel-gate lesson compounds: every probe is only as good as the closure that feeds it, and every closure matcher gap is a future manufacture. |
| C-38 | 2026-09-04 | **The §5.12 oracle pin named the ENGINE but not the TREE — and the two diverged the moment a tree-dependent fixture landed.** The CI oracle image (pinned by digest) was scheme-PARTIAL: same pdfTeX binary as the pin, but no `xy.tex`/`xypic.tex` — so `fr_t2_tree_input` graded strong-fatal against a tree that did not match the full-scheme tree the 199-root ground truth was recorded on, and `\include{xy}` of the then-missing file silently COMPILED (a missing-target `\include` is a no-op warning). The required tex-oracle gate caught it (hard=1) — the gate working exactly as designed. Fix: digest moved to the FULL-scheme TL2026 image (engine byte-identical, assert unchanged), and the canary now COMPILES every `Texmf_tree_allowlist` name before grading, so an under-provisioned tree can never grade silently. Lesson: an oracle is an engine PLUS a tree; pin both. |
| C-39 | 2026-09-04 | **I wrote the measurement instrument wrong TWICE in one sweep, and both errors flattered or alarmed the result.** (1) The sample-2 grader prompt said *"up to 3 passes (stop early if a pass exits nonzero)"* — the EXACT one-pass defect `run_to_fixpoint`'s own docstring exists to correct (natbib papers fail pass 1 and compile on pass 2). It manufactured **6 phantom FALSE-READYs** out of 16 claimed, and hid 2 real over-rejections inside `true-NOT-READY`. Caught because the autopsy layer re-verifies every cardinal cell from scratch: `2507.08223v1` came back `cell_confirmed=false` citing OPEN-003 by name. (2) The merge then compared `pdflatex_verdict == "compiles"` while graders had written `"compiles (A rc0, PDF; B rc0, PDF)"` — 21 true-READY papers momentarily scored FALSE-READY. Both were caught only because the numbers were implausible and got re-derived. **Rules going forward: a sweep prompt that re-states an existing protocol must QUOTE the implementation, not paraphrase it; and any grading field that a fleet fills in free text must be normalised at the boundary, never string-compared.** |
| C-40 | 2026-09-04 | **The single-source-of-truth document contradicted itself, and five other numbers were published in two places at once.** Live on main: PROJECT_STATE's hand-written §2 matrix (`141/199`) vs its own generated block (`197/199`) fifty lines above; the fixture-baseline row counting 14 accept-pins — fixtures pdflatex ACCEPTS, where READY is correct — as known false-READYs (29 published, 15 real); README titled v27.1.61 while `project_facts` said v27.1.62; `specs/rules/README.md` maturity stale by an order of magnitude (Draft 619/Impl 19 vs 529/108); "37 compile-blocking rules" in the guarantee doc against a code list of 36. Worse, the generated block published three EMPIRICAL rates and **never the North-Star metric itself**, which is why eight consecutive PRs optimised a proxy. Fixed: the metric (proven-verdict coverage, LP-Core and any-tier, both samples) is now GENERATED from committed per-document artefacts; row (b) uses the manifest's own baseline rule; prose points at the block instead of restating it; `check_doc_consistency.py` (required, in `spec-drift`, with two registered kill-tests) enforces five cross-document invariants. **Rule: a number lives in exactly ONE artefact — prose either points at it or is generated from it.** |
| C-41 | 2026-09-04 | **#570 introduced a false-READY channel that silences the ENTIRE compile-blocking belt, and I shipped it after an adversarial pass that never tested the interaction.** #570 moved T0's LP-tier classification onto the comment-BLANKED view (correct — a commented-out foreign construct is dead), but left `Language_profile.Context` — which drives `Validators._filter_by_tier` — holding the RAW-view tier. Since **zero of the 660 rule contracts carry `Any_tier`**, an LP-Foreign context drops every contracted rule. Net effect, measured: a document with an unclosed `\textbf{` argument (pdflatex rc 1,1, no PDF) is correctly NOT-READY; add ONE commented-out `\catcode` line and the identical document returns **READY**. | C-19 | A null field in a recorded artefact means the data is missing. | `declared_texlive` was null on all 200 rows and read as "arXiv does not publish it". arXiv publishes it on **1,880 of 2,821** packages; we were reading the wrong path (`process.texlive_version` instead of the top level). The null was OUR bug, described in the ledger as a property of the corpus. Worse, the field's absence had been used to excuse an unanswered question — and once answered it **refuted** the hypothesis it was supposed to support (5.4% vs 5.8%). Before recording "the data does not exist", read one raw source file. | Opening a single `00README.json`. |
| C-18 | #545 closed the `\verb`/`\lstinline` delimiter family. | It fixed the `[` spelling, **introduced a verbatim-corrupting regression** on `\verb[` (a legal delimiter that it treated as an optional argument), and left `\lstinline{…}` — the MOST COMMON spelling, 30 of 67 corpus uses against the 22 it fixed — carrying the identical over-reach it set out to remove. Net: one pass, one real fix, one new corruption, one missed majority. A fix aimed at one spelling of a construct must be re-measured against the **distribution** of that construct before the item is called closed. | Re-reading the ledger row for a supposedly-closed item, then running a delimiter histogram over the corpus instead of trusting the row. |
| C-17 | A broad `except Exception` around a file read is harmless defensive coding. | It swallowed a `NameError` (`json` was never imported) and reported **"no measured_at_sha"** — a plausible, entirely wrong diagnostic that sent me inspecting the data instead of the code. Catch only what can genuinely go wrong; a broad except converts a programming error into a convincing finding. | The gate reporting a field missing that I could see was present. |

---

## 5. Process invariants

Earned, each at a cost. Violating one is how the entries in §4 happened.

1. **Grade every corpus against the oracle, once, when you add it.** OPEN-001 exists because a corpus was added to test lint firing and never compiled.
2. **A verdict flip is not a win until pdflatex agrees.** (C-5)
3. **Verify a gate in BOTH directions** — clean pass *and* injected failure, naming the expected signal.
4. **Never trust a background run without a completion sentinel.** (C-9)
5. **Never set a latency threshold on this machine.** Load has been observed at 158–230. Interleaved A/B at load ≤ 8 only, and CI and this laptop compile different C tokenizers.
6. **Quote (c), or quote nothing.** (a) and (b) grade toy corpora.
7. **Edit `.github/required-status-checks.json`, never the branch-protection API** — `branch-protection.yml` PUTs the file on every push to main.
8. **Verify PR state via `statusCheckRollup`**, never by reading the check list.
9. **Order a release: CHANGELOG + ROADMAP prose first, `release.sh` second.**
10. **A gate nobody has seen fail is not a gate — and this is now MACHINERY, not a rule to remember.** `check_gate_selftests.py` (required, spec-drift + build) applies known-bad mutations to gate inputs — each in a disposable, proven-identical worktree copy, in parallel — and asserts each covered gate fails WITH its expected message; the registry pins its own size, every anchor must match exactly once, and every `check_*.py` wired into spec-drift must be covered or carry a written exemption. Invariant 3 above, enforced mechanically. (OPEN-036)
11. **A published number must be recomputable from the artefact that carries it.** `results.json`'s `APPLIED TO k/n` protocol clause is recomputed from per-row `pdflatex_passes` on every `check_project_state` run (C-28); the differential's denominator is derived by the SAME enumeration rule the differential uses, pinned by an assertion (C-29). When you publish a claim, publish its derivation next to it and gate the two together.
12. **The oracle is the NEWEST STABLE TeX Live, rolled forward annually as a gated event — never via continuous `tlmgr` tracking.** Owner decision, 2026-08-25, with the TL2024 control on the table. Rationale: user engines are heterogeneous, so no single oracle is "correct"; you pick the one whose errors are least harmful. A false-READY under the newest engine bites a current-install author on their own machine (the cardinal bug, delivered personally); an over-rejection under the newest engine is soft, because the message can be version-conditioned ("fails under TL≥2026; compiles under earlier; fix: load amsthm after thmtools"). Fresh installs only move forward, so the newest-engine population grows monotonically. Version-sensitivity is DIAGNOSTIC METADATA, not an excuse: the TL2024 differential enriches messages, it does not move the pin backwards. The annual roll re-grades all corpora (STRICT_GRADE re-record) — which also catches version-sensitive classes healing as the ecosystem patches (the `\c@` class is likely transient). The l3backend incident is why the roll is never `tlmgr update`.

---

## 6. Provenance

The generated block is regenerated by `scripts/tools/gen_project_state.py` and
verified by `scripts/tools/check_project_state.py`, which runs in the **required**
`spec-drift` job. The hand-written sections are the author's responsibility; every
claim in them is either marked verified with its evidence, or marked UNVERIFIED.
